- [x] **AK-DTREE-2** (operator directive 2026-11-19; DTREE-P3/P4/P5 kernel side) — faithful Infernix-style tree verification kernels for the 27B (`model: Qwen3.8-27B` + DFlash2 drafter) and the GPU A3B (MTP). Branch `llama.cpp-experimental-dtree-kernels-20261119` on `fork` from C14 `eee3da838`; worktree `/mnt/raid0/llm/worktrees/llama-dtree-kernels-20261119`. Reference: Infernix @ `397f8cd1cc64` `docs/maintainer/tree-verification.md` (Apache-2.0, credited in commits). **Design (ggml mapping):** ✅ 2026-11-22 [belief: cc] fail; 2a-2e done, P5 negative on 27B and A3B (AK-DTREE-2d); tree track PARKED per docs/design/dtree-v5i-round-budget-20261122.md.
  - **Attention needs NO new kernel.** FLASH_ATTN_EXT takes an arbitrary f16 KQ mask `[n_kv, n_batch_pad]`; Infernix's rule (key at cache row k visible to column c iff k < F or bit k-F of c's ancestor mask is set) is just a different mask fill. The work is in `llama-kv-cache` `set_input_kq_mask`: cells written by the current tree ubatch use ancestor-of instead of pos<=pos (siblings share a depth/pos). RoPE position = anchor pos + depth (already a per-token pos in `llama_batch`).
  - **SSM_CONV:** new `ggml_ssm_conv_tree(sx, c, parent)`; `parent` I32 `[n_t, n_s]` in `src[2]`, `parent[t]` in `[-1, t-1]`. Window of t = its d_conv-1 nearest ancestors (conv history once the walk passes the root) then t, summed in the chain's tap order.
  - **GATED_DELTA_NET (ReplaySSM record mode):** new `ggml_gated_delta_net_tree(q,k,v,g,beta,state,parent)`; `parent` in `src[6]`. Each column continues from its parent's state; K = n_tokens snapshot slots with the chain mapping (slot n_tokens-1-c = state after column c). The input state is never written, so rejected branches need no rollback, and **fold = slot select** of the last accepted column (the same mechanism as today's `n_rs_seq` rollback slots, indexed by column instead of by tokens-back). Our recurrent kernel gives each warp one state column, so the walk reloads only the shard the warp itself stored: no shared-memory branch slots needed (Infernix's 2-slot nesting is a CUDA-tile artefact).
  - **Graph/memory interface for AK-DTREE-1's switch-over (P5):** `llama_batch` gains a nullable `int32_t * parent` (in-batch parent index, -1 = child of the committed context; tokens of one tree contiguous per seq, parent < index; null = chain, all current callers unchanged). In tree ubatches `build_conv_state` / `build_recurrent_attn` use the tree ops and write per-column snapshots into W rollback slots (requires `n_rs_seq + 1 >= W`), the conv-state snapshot of column c being its ancestor window (get_rows over `conv_input`). New `llama_tree_accept(ctx, seq_id, const int32_t * cols, n)`: KV frees the round's non-accepted cells (holes are fine; accepted cells already carry depth positions, so no KV compaction copy), recurrent memory copies the slot of the last accepted column into the live state. The spec layer then swaps its path-per-seq_id batch for one tree batch + `llama_tree_accept` behind `--draft-tree-mode batch` (reserved by P1 `6ad10b3bc`; `common_dtree_flatten` already yields the parent-index view this batch takes).
  - **Known gap:** Metal/Vulkan/SYCL/OpenCL/WebGPU supports_op do not check the new `src[]` and would silently compute the chain op; not built here (CPU + HIP only). Guard them before any upstreaming.

  - 2026-11-20: **T9 root cause found and fixed. It was not the path-row code.** The AK-ASM-H15 CUDA fusion (concat + conv-state cpy + ssm_conv + silu + l2_norm q/k, `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/ak-fused.cu`) writes silu/l2q/l2k from concurrently running blocks while other blocks still read the conv state `st` and `qkv`. The allocator may place those outputs over those inputs, because in the unfused graph `st`/`qkv` die at CONCAT. Logged on 2- and 3-sequence path-row ubatches: silu == st, l2q/l2k inside qkv, which races and corrupts the conv state. Evidence: CPU is clean. On GPU with Qwen3.5-0.8B and a varying 2/3-path schedule, `test-spec-dtree-verify` fails (22-33 tokens >0.5 nat off a fresh prefill, max 18 nat). It is clean with `GGML_CUDA_AK_H15=0` or `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`. It still fails with `GGML_HIP_Q8_WVSK=0`/`_MAX=16`, with CUDA graphs off and with graph reuse off, so wvsk is ruled out. Fix: fork commit `f1d8f383f` on `llama.cpp-experimental-dtree-20261119` rejects the fusion on any output/input overlap (reject 14, unfused fallback). Its regression test is the `test-spec-dtree-verify` oracle/schedule modes (`<model> 128 8 4 -1`, `DTREE_NGL=99`): FAIL before, OK after. A 27B T9 rerun on the fixed build gave 0/128 oracle disagreements. Production v10 (`ffc1bac82`) has no H15. The C14 champion store (contains `bd950e2dc` H15) is exposed on any multi-sequence ubatch (np>1); the L arms are np1. r2 published: `ak-dtree-27b-gpu-20261119-r2` (AMENDMENT 3). [no-belief: diagnosis, r2 verdict pending]
  - **Note to the AK-DTREE-2 kernels-branch owner (2026-11-20):** `llama.cpp-experimental-dtree-kernels-20261119` @ `416e5ac5f` carries the same unfixed H15 (`ak-fused.cu` is identical to the dtree base). Batch mode is one sequence, but the race depends on the allocator layout, not on the sequence count; nt <= 32 tree ubatches take the fused path. Cherry-pick or forward-port `f1d8f383f` (one file, `ak-fused.cu`) before any batch-mode GPU identity or oracle row. Otherwise a batch-mode oracle failure can be this bug and not the tree kernels. The CHAMPION also needs the fix (np>1 serving).

Management rule: each phase's subagent hands back to autokernel, which files the result, ticks the box with evidence and dispatches the next phase in the same turn. Blockers become fix tasks, not operator questions.

**Intake refinements 2026-11-19 (research intake intake-2705..intake-2717; Infernix read at `397f8cd`, file:line cites are `docs/maintainer/tree-verification.md` = TV).** These amend P1-P6. Where they conflict with the boxes above, these notes win.
- *The MI210 breakeven decides everything. Read it first.* Our own DF2-VERIFY-SLOPE row (2026-11-09, above) measured s = 2.39 ms per extra verify row on 27B Q8_0, against a 52.5 ms chain round (flat batches of 8-16 rows at d512), and futility-closed the earlier hybrid-tree port. That is about 4.6 % of round time per added column. Infernix's 5090 figure is about 1.2 %: +0.74 ms for 4 columns on a 14.6 ms round (TV:L575, L591). With Infernix's acceptance gains (+12 % at 12 columns, +17 % at 16; TV:L551-552), MI210 at today's slope pays about +18 % / +36 % round time, which is a net loss at both widths. Breakeven needs the marginal row cost at or below about 1.3-1.5 ms (12 columns). So the program's first deliverable is **slope reduction**: find out why one extra row costs 2.39 ms on a bandwidth-bound decode (batched MMVQ/MMQ switch-over, GDN record, LM head over W columns), and cut it. That also helps chain spec. Reuse the untested tree code on `llama.cpp-experimental-c10-dftree-20261109` @ `84934b297` (lattice tree policy, replay tests 12/12) instead of rewriting P1. LumoTree (intake-2716), on the same Qwen3.8-27B with a 27-candidate MTP tree on GB10, reports tree verify reaching only the chain stack's rate. It calls that the cost of hosting a tree, not a gain from it.
- *Gain is acceptance, not speed.* The "+12-17 %" in issue #30 is accepted drafts per round (intake-2706#00). On RTX 5090, 27B NVFP4, DFlash2 K=7, T=0.6, the decode tok/s gain is smaller and shrinks with concurrency: C=1 +6.9 % at 12 columns and +8.6 % at 16; C=2 +3.0 %; C=4 -0.9 % at 9 columns (TV:L525-537). Long context costs more: +5-6 % at 16K, +1-2 % at 64K, -3-4 % at 128K, mostly from verify attention (TV:L609-617). Size the P2/P5 expectations from these, and include context buckets (<=8K, 32K, 64K) in the P5 row, not only short prompts.
- *Greedy identity gate as written will fail for a correct build.* A tree round widens the verify block, which selects different GEMM tiles and attention routes, so its main-chain columns are not bit-identical to a chain round's (TV:L225-227). Infernix instead compares every token against a fresh one-token greedy prefill of the committed prefix. Disagreement was 3.3 % for 8-column chains, 3.0 % for 16-column chains and 3.3 % for 16-column trees (TV:L397-406). Replace "token-identical to linear spec" in P1/P2/P5 with: the tree arm's oracle disagreement rate is <= the chain arm's + 0.5 points on the same prompts, and no disagreement has a margin above 0.5 nat that the chain arm lacks. Bit-exactness stays the gate for the op level only (P3/P4: each tree path's GDN record equals the chain record on that path).
- *Build the tree from our own DFlash2 lattice, best first.* Our DFlash2 selector already emits the predecessor-conditional lattice `[ids | scores[p][c]]` (top-k 16) in `h_nextn`, plus per-position sampling dists (`common/speculative.cpp:1780-1820 @ eee3da838`). That is the same input Infernix's builder uses (TV:L112-135). P1 should build from that lattice, not from independent per-position top-k: main chain first (bit-identical to today's draft), then side columns best first by priority = path score x expected next-child law, side columns after the main chain, and depth-K nodes get no children.
- *Path rows cost about path count x chain.* Infernix uses path rows only where attention has no tree form (Flash-Next QSA). Each shared prefix is recomputed per path, so a round costs about its path count's chains, and it caps R x W at 16 columns (TV:L473-498). Two paths bought only +0.8 %; three or more bought about +6.4 % (TV:L578-582). For P1: use 3 paths of <=5 columns, or 2 of <=8, keep R x W <= 16, and treat a P2 loss as expected. P1 is a correctness scaffold and a lower bound; the ancestor-mask tree (P3-P5) is the real design.
- *Ancestor mask + compaction, not seq_cp, for P3/P5.* Use one ragged row: KV slot F+c, RoPE position = anchor + depth[c], and visibility = cache rows [0,F) plus the column's ancestor bits (TV:L176-192). Our FLASH_ATTN_EXT KQ mask can carry this as data, so the attention change may be mask construction only. Before commit, compact: copy accepted side column c_i onto column i for the KV slots, the recurrent record planes, the hidden state and the DFlash2 target features. The existing commit path then sees a chain round (TV:L312-328). The GDN conv window reads the column's 3 nearest ancestors from the *materialized* projection, so tree rounds cannot use a fused projection+conv (TV:L182, L194-198).
- *gfx90a kernel risks (P4).* The CUDA tree walk assumes warp 32 with a 16-row state tile made of 4 warps x 4 rows. Remap for wave64. LDS use is small: 16 KiB for the walk's 2 slots, 11.7 KiB for the closed form. The closed-form TreeWY route (arXiv 2608.20961, intake-2708) serves rows of <=16 columns. It is within BF16 rounding of the recurrence but not bit-exact (TV:L211-223), so if it is built, gate it on NMSE plus the oracle rule above. An alternative is commit-time path replay: verify read-only through an ancestor-masked triangular solve, then replay the accepted path at the next commit (Trees from Marginals, intake-2713, stage1-unverified, so its numbers stay out of plans). Its solve cost grows super-linearly with tree size, so P4 should time walk vs closed form vs solve at W = 8/12/16 on gfx90a before choosing.
- *A3B MTP trees are leaves-only.* MTP drafts autoregressively, so the tree is the chain plus side leaves taken from the head's top ranks; side columns hang only off chain columns (TV:L153-170).
- *Sampled rows need recursive rejection sampling.* Production samples, so lossless sampled tree acceptance must try children in draw order, drawn without replacement. Accept child j with probability min(1, r(x_j)/Q_j(x_j)), update the residual r = norm(max(r - Q_j, 0)), and emit a correction from r if every child is rejected (TV:L235-274; RSD, intake-2714). RSD exactness needs the siblings sampled without replacement from the draft law. If sampled rows ever take deterministic top-k siblings, use UniVer's mixed rule (one residual draw) instead. The simplest exact rule for any tree, deterministic siblings included, is the target-sampler walk: at each node, sample from the target and follow the child that matches, otherwise emit the sample (DDTree, intake-2667). It accepts less than residual sampling but needs no draft probabilities, so it is the P1 default for sampled rows. Penalty overlays (presence penalty 1.0 in production) must use each column's own root-to-node path history. A flattened history skews the distribution (LumoTree, intake-2716; Infernix TV:L232-233). Traversal Verification (intake-2717) adds only about 1.5 % acceptance at T 0.6, so it is not planned. Tree shape may depend only on earlier draws.

Gain is measured on the generated text: a narrower tree is a prefix of a wider one, so each wide round also yields every narrower width's tokens. Keep a decayed sum (1/512), counted only after 32 rows.
